DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player guides a constantly‑forward moving dot through an endless tunnel by tapping to shift all obstacles left or right, lining up with the open lane. The pleasure comes from quick, decisive lane shifts that keep the dot racing forward.

## Player
The avatar is a bright dot (or tiny ship) that always moves forward through the tunnel.
Verbs: shift (tap left/right to slide obstacles), dodge (avoid colliding lanes), keep moving (maintain forward momentum).

## Core loop
Every few seconds a new pattern of obstacles scrolls toward the player. The player reads the upcoming pattern, decides whether to tap left or right to shift the entire pattern, executes the tap, and the tunnel scrolls forward while the dot races through the newly aligned gap. This rapid read‑act‑react cycle repeats, driving momentum and tension.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- Solid lane blocks that fill one or two lanes for a short stretch, forcing a lane shift to survive.
- Dual‑lane walls that leave only a single lane open, demanding precise timing.
- Horizontal sweepers that travel across lanes within a segment, creating a moving barrier that must be avoided by shifting at the right moment.
- Burst spikes that appear briefly in a lane before vanishing, adding an extra timing challenge.

## Score
The score rises each time the player successfully passes an obstacle segment without collision. Bonus points are awarded for perfect timing when a shift aligns exactly with a narrow gap.

## Win and lose
The player loses instantly on any collision with an obstacle; a “Game Over” overlay appears showing the final score and restart instructions. The player wins by surviving a predefined distance or time, triggering a celebratory “You Win!” screen with a burst of neon.

## Feel
Frantic, high‑velocity arcade action with a neon‑lit tunnel that pulses to synth music. The aesthetic is sharp, glowing neon against a dark backdrop, with crisp sound effects for each shift and collision.

## Why it's fun
The instant, satisfying snap of shifting lanes to thread a dot through ever‑tighter gaps creates a rush that makes players grin, curse, or shout with each close call.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold and meta.json – create index.html with required header, canvas, restart button, empty script block, and a matching meta.json with title, description, controls, tags.
2. Game state objects – define player, hazards array, score, phase/state, and constants (canvas size, lanes, safe radius) at top of script.
3. Animation loop – implement startLoop() with cancelAnimationFrame discipline, dt calculation, try/catch, calling update(dt) and render() each frame.
4. Input handling – set up a Set of pressed keys, keydown/keyup listeners for left/right arrows (or A/D) and R key, plus click listener on restart button; both invoke a reset() function.
5. Player movement – apply dt‑based horizontal acceleration from left/right inputs, friction, clamp position within canvas bounds, and expose player.x/y/visible.
6. Hazard system – create hazard objects with lane position and width, spawn timer with safe‑radius check, and dt‑scaled downward motion.
7. Collision detection – check player against hazards each frame; on hit set state to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay loop.
8. Scoring – increment score when a hazard passes the player without collision, and draw the current score on the canvas.
9. Rendering – clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player (high‑contrast neon dot), and HUD elements each frame.
10. Game‑over overlay – dim the canvas and render "Game Over" with final score and restart instructions.
11. Observable contract – populate window.__game each frame with state ("playing"|"gameover"), score, player position/visibility, and a one‑sentence objective description.
12. Audio – add a lazy‑init beep() helper using AudioContext, wrapped in try/catch, and trigger sounds on score, shift, and death events.

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Lane Shift</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Guide a dot through an endless tunnel by shifting lanes to avoid obstacles. Tap left or right to move obstacles and keep the dot moving forward.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap left/right to shift lanes</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    const CANVAS_WIDTH = 800;
    const CANVAS_HEIGHT = 600;
    const LANE_COUNT = 3;
    const LANE_WIDTH = CANVAS_WIDTH / LANE_COUNT;
    const SAFE_RADIUS = 150; // pixels, safe start area

    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

    let player = { x: CANVAS_WIDTH / 2, y: CANVAS_HEIGHT - 50, lane: 1 };
    let hazards = []; // array of obstacle objects
    let score = 0;
    let phase = "menu"; // "playing" | "gameover"
    let keys = new Set(); // will hold pressed keys
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    let loopId = null;
    let audioCtx = null;

    // === LOOP =============================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error('loop error', err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    function update(dt) {
      if (phase !== "playing") return;

      // Input handling: lane shift on key press
      if (keys.has('left')) {
        player.lane = Math.max(0, player.lane - 1);
        keys.delete('left');
        beep(440, 0.05);
      }
      if (keys.has('right')) {
        player.lane = Math.min(LANE_COUNT - 1, player.lane + 1);
        keys.delete('right');
        beep(440, 0.05);
      }

      // Update player position based on lane
      player.x = player.lane * LANE_WIDTH + LANE_WIDTH / 2;

      // Hazard spawning
      spawnTimer -= dt;
      if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
        // random lane hazard
        const lane = Math.floor(Math.random() * LANE_COUNT);
        hazards.push({
          lane,
          x: lane * LANE_WIDTH + (LANE_WIDTH - HAZARD_WIDTH) / 2,
          y: -HAZARD_HEIGHT,
          width: HAZARD_WIDTH,
          height: HAZARD_HEIGHT,
        });
        // Decrease interval as score grows (difficulty ramp)
        spawnTimer = Math.max(0.5, 1.5 - score * 0.02);
      }

      // Move hazards and check collisions / scoring
      for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        const h = hazards[i];
        h.y += HAZARD_SPEED * dt;
        // Collision with player
        if (h.lane === player.lane &&
            h.y + h.height >= player.y - PLAYER_RADIUS &&
            h.y <= player.y + PLAYER_RADIUS) {
          gameOver();
          return;
        }
        // Passed bottom -> score
        if (h.y > CANVAS_HEIGHT) {
          score += SCORE_PER_HAZARD;
          beep(660, 0.05);
          hazards.splice(i, 1);
        }
      }
    }

    function render() {
      // TODO: implement rendering logic
    }

    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") {
        reset();
        return;
      }
      if (ev.key === "ArrowLeft" || ev.key === "a" || ev.key === "A") {
        keys.add("left");
      } else if (ev.key === "ArrowRight" || ev.key === "d" || ev.key === "D") {
        keys.add("right");
      }
    });
    document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === "ArrowLeft" || ev.key === "a" || ev.key === "A") {
        keys.delete("left");
      } else if (ev.key === "ArrowRight" || ev.key === "d" || ev.key === "D") {
        keys.delete("right");
      }
    });
    restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      player = { x: CANVAS_WIDTH / 2, y: CANVAS_HEIGHT - 50, lane: 1 };
      hazards = [];
      score = 0;
      phase = "playing";
      keys.clear();
      lastTime = performance.now();
      startLoop();
    }
  </script>
</body>
</html>

Title: Lane Shift

Concept: Player controls a dot (or simple shape) traveling forward through a tunnel divided into three vertical lanes. Obstacles appear in lanes. Tap left/right to shift all obstacles one lane left/right, aligning the dot with the safe lane. The tension is aligning at the right moment.

We need to keep abstract; not mention coordinates or speeds.

Player: The player controls a simple dot (or a ship) that moves forward automatically. The only player inputs are taps / swipes to shift obstacles left or right.

Verbs: shift, dodge, maybe "slide"? We'll list as "shift obstacles left", "shift obstacles right". Maybe "tap" as input.

Core loop: The tunnel scrolls forward, obstacles appear in lanes in patterns. Player watches tunnel, sees upcoming obstacles, decides to shift left or right to align with open lane. Each decision occurs every short interval; success leads to continued forward motion, failure leads to collision.

Hazards/Obstacles: stationary blocks that fill a lane; moving blockers that sweep across multiple lanes; temporary gaps; locked doors that require multiple shifts; etc.

Score: Increase for passing each obstacle column; combo for consecutive successful shifts; collectable items; maybe bonus for perfect runs.

Win and lose: Lose when collision with obstacle, screen flashes, game ends. Win maybe after surviving a set distance/time or reaching end of a level. Or infinite runner where high score is goal.

Feel: Fast-paced, rhythmic, neon retro aesthetic. Vibe: adrenaline rush, satisfying timing.

Why it's fun: The single moment of decision: you see the lanes diverge and must quickly shift, generating a "just in time" feeling.

Now implement update:

function update(dt) { if (phase !== 'playing') return; // handle input lane shift; move hazards, spawn, collision detection, scoring }

Let's script:

```
function update(dt) {
  // handle input only once per press
  if (keys.has('left')) {
    player.lane = Math.max(0, player.lane - 1);
    keys.delete('left');
    beep(440, 0.05);
  }
  if (keys.has('right')) {
    player.lane = Math.min(LANE_COUNT - 1, player.lane + 1);
    keys.delete('right');
    beep(440, 0.05);
  }
  // update player position
  player.x = player.lane * LANE_WIDTH + LANE_WIDTH / 2;
  // spawn hazards
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    // spawn hazard in random lane
    const lane = Math.floor(Math.random() * LANE_COUNT);
    hazards.push({
      lane,
      x: lane * LANE_WIDTH + (LANE_WIDTH - HAZARD_WIDTH) / 2,
      y: -HAZARD_HEIGHT,
      width: HAZARD_WIDTH,
      height: HAZARD_HEIGHT,
    });
    // reset timer, can decrease as score increases for difficulty
    spawnTimer = Math.max(0.5, 1.5 - score * 0.02);
  }
  // move hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.y += HAZARD_SPEED * dt;
    // collision detection
    if (h.lane === player.lane && h.y + h.height >= player.y - PLAYER_RADIUS && h.y <= player.y + PLAYER_RADIUS) {
      gameOver();
      return;
    }
    // passed player (off bottom)
    if (h.y > CANVAS_HEIGHT) {
      score += SCORE_PER_HAZARD;
      beep(660, 0.05);
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
    }
  }
}
```

Define gameOver function:

```
function gameOver() {
  phase = 'gameover';
  beep(120, 0.2);
  if (loopId !== null) {
    cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    loopId = null;
  }
}
```

Now render function:

```
function render() {
  // clear
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // draw lane dividers
  ctx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(125,249,255,0.1)';
  ctx.lineWidth = 2;
  for (let i = 1; i < LANE_COUNT; i++) {
    const x = i * LANE_WIDTH;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(x, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(x, CANVAS_HEIGHT);
    ctx.stroke();
  }
  // draw hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
  hazards.forEach(h => {
    ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.y, h.width, h.height);
  });
  // draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = '#ff00aa';
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
  // HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
  // game over overlay
  if (phase === 'gameover') {
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(10,10,15,0.7)';
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
    ctx.textAlign = 'center';
    ctx.font = '44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillText('Game Over', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
    ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
    ctx.font = '20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
    ctx.fillStyle = '#a5a5b8';
    ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillText('Press R or click Restart', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
  }
}
```

}

```
// === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = 'square';
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {}
}
```

Implementation of beep function:

```
let audioCtx = null;
function beep(freq, dur) {
  if (!audioCtx) {
    audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
  }
  const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
  const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
  osc.frequency.value = freq;
  osc.connect(gain);
  gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
  gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.1, audioCtx.currentTime);
  osc.start();
  osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + dur);
}
```

Add after audioCtx.

```
function render() {
  // clear canvas
  ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

  // draw background (optional)
  ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

  // draw hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff6666";
  for (const h of hazards) {
    ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.y, h.width, h.height);
  }

  // draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();

  // draw score
  ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
  ctx.font = "16px sans-serif";
  ctx.textAlign = "left";
  ctx.textBaseline = "top";
  ctx.fillText(`Score: ${score}`, 10, 10);
}
```

prompt (12,547 chars)
## Reference example: "snake"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json

}
```

  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const CELL = 25;
  const COLS = 32;            // 32 * 25 = 800
  const ROWS = 24;            // 24 * 25 = 600
  const TICK_START_MS = 140;  // snake step interval at game start
  const TICK_MIN_MS = 60;     // floor speed
  const TICK_SPEEDUP = 3;     // ms shaved off per food eaten

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let snake;          // array of { x, y } in grid coords; snake[0] is head
  let dir;            // { x, y } unit vector
  let nextDir;        // queued direction (so quick double-taps don't U-turn)
  let food;           // { x, y } in grid coords
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let stepIntervalMs;
  let stepAccumulatorMs;
  let phase;          // "playing" | "gameover"
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    snake = [{ x: 10, y: 12 }, { x: 9, y: 12 }, { x: 8, y: 12 }];
    dir = { x: 1, y: 0 };
    nextDir = dir;
    food = randomFreeCell();
    score = 0;
    stepIntervalMs = TICK_START_MS;
    stepAccumulatorMs = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    stepAccumulatorMs += dt * 1000;
    while (stepAccumulatorMs >= stepIntervalMs) {
      stepAccumulatorMs -= stepIntervalMs;
      step();
      if (phase !== "playing") return;
    }
  }

  function step() {
    // Commit queued direction; reject reversals.
    if (nextDir.x !== -dir.x || nextDir.y !== -dir.y) dir = nextDir;

    const head = snake[0];
    const nx = head.x + dir.x;
    const ny = head.y + dir.y;

    // Wall collision -> game over.
    if (nx < 0 || nx >= COLS || ny < 0 || ny >= ROWS) {
      gameOver();
      return;
    }
    // Self collision (skip last segment because it's about to move).
    for (let i = 0; i < snake.length - 1; i++) {
      if (snake[i].x === nx && snake[i].y === ny) {
        gameOver();
        return;
      }
    }

    snake.unshift({ x: nx, y: ny });

    if (nx === food.x && ny === food.y) {
      score++;
      stepIntervalMs = Math.max(TICK_MIN_MS, stepIntervalMs - TICK_SPEEDUP);
      food = randomFreeCell();
      beep(660, 0.06);
    } else {
      snake.pop();
    }
  }

  function gameOver() {
    phase = "gameover";
    if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
    beep(120, 0.18);
  }

  function randomFreeCell() {
    while (true) {
      const c = { x: Math.floor(Math.random() * COLS), y: Math.floor(Math.random() * ROWS) };
      if (!snake.some((s) => s.x === c.x && s.y === c.y)) return c;
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    // Background grid.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "rgba(125,249,255,0.04)";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1;
    for (let x = 0; x <= COLS; x++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(x * CELL, 0); ctx.lineTo(x * CELL, ROWS * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    for (let y = 0; y <= ROWS; y++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(0, y * CELL); ctx.lineTo(COLS * CELL, y * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }

    // Food (pulsing).
    const pulse = 0.85 + 0.15 * Math.sin(performance.now() / 180);
    drawCell(food.x, food.y, `rgba(255,82,174,${pulse.toFixed(3)})`);

    // Snake body.
    for (let i = snake.length - 1; i >= 1; i--) {
      drawCell(snake[i].x, snake[i].y, "#52ffae");
    }
    // Snake head — brighter so the player tracks it instantly.
    drawCell(snake[0].x, snake[0].y, "#9dffd1");

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}` + (score === highScore && score > 0 ? " — new best!" : ""), canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
    }
  }

  function drawCell(cx, cy, fill) {
    ctx.fillStyle = fill;
    ctx.fillRect(cx * CELL + 1, cy * CELL + 1, CELL - 2, CELL - 2);
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    switch (ev.key) {
      case "ArrowUp": case "w": case "W": nextDir = { x: 0, y: -1 }; break;
      case "ArrowDown": case "s": case "S": nextDir = { x: 0, y: 1 }; break;
      case "ArrowLeft": case "a": case "A": nextDir = { x: -1, y: 0 }; break;
      case "ArrowRight": case "d": case "D": nextDir = { x: 1, y: 0 }; break;
    }
  });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    const head = snake[0];
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                     // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: head.x * CELL + CELL / 2,    // canvas-space center
        y: head.y * CELL + CELL / 2,
        visible: head.x >= 0 && head.x < COLS && head.y >= 0 && head.y < ROWS,
      },
      objective: "Eat the pink food. Don't hit the walls or your own tail.",
    };
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:lane-shift-2:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

```js
document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
  if (ev.key === "ArrowLeft" || ev.key === "a" || ev.key === "A") { keys.add("left"); }
  if (ev.key === "ArrowRight" || ev.key === "d" || ev.key === "D") { keys.add("right"); }
});
document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === "ArrowLeft" || ev.key === "a" || ev.key === "A") { keys.delete("left"); }
  if (ev.key === "ArrowRight" || ev.key === "d" || ev.key === "D") { keys.delete("right"); }
});
restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);
```

    }

    document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {

        reset();

        keys.add("right");
      }
    });
    document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === "ArrowLeft" || ev.key === "a" || ev.key === "A") {
        keys.delete("left");
      } else if (ev.key === "ArrowRight" || ev.key === "d" || ev.key === "D") {
        keys.delete("right");
      }
    });
    restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      player = { x: CANVAS_WIDTH / 2, y: CANVAS_HEIGHT - 50, lane: 1 };
      hazards = [];
      score = 0;
      phase = "playing";
      keys.clear();
      lastTime = performance.now();

Implement beep as:

function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = "square";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    // silent fail
  }
}

Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Lane Shift
Concept: The tunnel is divided into three vertical lanes that scroll forward; each tap slides all obstacles one lane left or right, letting the dot align with the open lane as it races forward.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
